Cleanup done (probe tables dropped; the remaining server on port 8095 is the user's own daemon, untouched). Arbitration evidence is complete: I verified on the exact PB 0.39.11 binary that FTS5 works, bind-param `MATCH` works via the `DynamicModel`/`arrayOf` pattern, prefix search works, adversarial input crashes with an `fts5: syntax error`, and bare `.all()` fails with "Invalid variable type: must be a pointer".

```json
{
  "winner": "Option A — dedicated SQLite FTS5 virtual table (workspace_fts) + sync-metadata table, maintained incrementally by record hooks, with idempotent rebuild route, hydration guard, and sanitized prefix-token queries",
  "confidence": 0.72,
  "key_reasons": [
    "All three participating debaters converged on Option A after rebuttal; Option C is ruled out by the FTS5 mandate and is exactly what the current 94_workspace_synthesis.pb.js does (capped findRecordsByFilter scans of 300/200/1000/500 per collection); Option B carries the same hook-drift exposure as A with double maintenance and no benefit since FTS5 is confirmed present.",
    "Empirically verified on the actual projectbase PB 0.39.11 binary (isolated scratch instance): CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE USING fts5 and INSERT work via app.db().newQuery().execute(); parameterized MATCH works via .bind({term}) with {:term} placeholders; prefix search ('bet*') works.",
    "Critical implementation gotcha found by arbitration, missed in the debate: bare newQuery().all()/.one()/.rows() throw 'Invalid variable type: must be a pointer' in this build; FTS5 reads MUST use arrayOf(new DynamicModel({...})) models. This explains why existing repo code reads only via findRecordsByFilter and must be baked into the Epic 11 API design and tests.",
    "Adversarial input ('AND OR NOT \" * ( ) : ^') empirically produces 'fts5: syntax error near AND', proving sanitized tokenization with quoted prefix terms ('\"term\"*') is mandatory, not optional.",
    "The hook-drift critique (bulk imports, direct SQL, cascades bypassing hooks) is real but not architecture-fatal: treat the index as a cache, verify hydration via findRecordById, add a drift watermark/count-hash consistency check, and provide transactional idempotent rebuild (startup + authenticated route).",
    "Auth scoping pre-query (project_id filter) plus post-hydration verification prevents cross-project leakage; MCP parity via shared handlers; no external deps keeps footprint under the ~50MB goal."
  ],
  "consensus": [
    "Option A architecture: dedicated FTS5 virtual table + metadata table, incremental hook maintenance, rebuild/repair route, hydration guard, source records remain the single source of truth.",
    "FTS5 is available in the PB 0.39.11 embedded SQLite driver (table creation verified in-migration and at runtime).",
    "Query safety via sanitized alphanumeric tokens quoted as prefix terms is required (empirically confirmed).",
    "Index drift is inevitable with any hook-maintained derived table (A and B equally) and must be detectable and repairable rather than absolutely prevented.",
    "Acceptance tests via test_workspace_synthesis.py covering performance, adversarial queries, project scoping, cycle detection, and REST/MCP parity."
  ],
  "divergence": [
    "Option B (ordinary workspace_documents as source of truth, FTS5 optional acceleration): rejected because FTS5 is confirmed available and B duplicates maintenance while sharing the same drift exposure.",
    "Drift-detection mechanism: Gemini favors hydration guard + startup rebuild; GLM favors contentless/external-content table with count/hash consistency check; DeepSeek favors a sync watermark counter. Complementary; the minimal combination is watermark + hydration.",
    "Acceptance numbers differ: Gemini proposes 10k records <3s index, <25MB growth, <15ms latency, while the Epic 11 context targets ~50MB total footprint; treat both as unbenchmarked targets to reconcile in tests.",
    "GPT 5.6 Sol High failed in Round 1 (model call error), contributing no position or critique, so Round 2/3 coverage is from 3 of 4 models."
  ],
  "next_validation": [
    "Implement the minimal Epic 11 migration creating workspace_fts USING fts5(record_id UNINDEXED, collection UNINDEXED, project_id UNINDEXED, title, body) plus workspace_fts_meta, one hook, and the rebuild route; then run test_workspace_synthesis.py exercising parameterized MATCH via the DynamicModel/arrayOf read pattern (not bare .all()).",
    "Benchmark on 10k mixed records: index build time (<3s), DB size growth (<25MB, total <50MB), p95 search latency (<15ms).",
    "Verify cascade-delete behavior (does onRecordAfterDelete fire for child issues/comments when a project is deleted) and confirm rebuild-route idempotence on repeated invocation."
  ],
  "uncertainty_flags": [

    "PB cascade-delete hook firing and bulk-import hook coverage remain untested; the rebuild route is the safety net.",
```

## Decision for human gate

- **Recommended:** derive from arbiter JSON `winner` and confidence
- **Do NOT relitigate:** items listed in debate context block
- **If INCONCLUSIVE:** do not treat as approval; rerun once or escalate to human with transcript
- **Invalid for gate:** `winner: null`, `arbiter_failure`, or status ≠ COMPLETE

## Handoff

- **To inspect:** `flow_debate.py --validate` must return valid=true (COMPLETE + non-null winner)
- **To HUMAN_REQUIRED:** link this file + summarize consensus/divergence for the pending decision
